Proof A.10
.

Let
t_{0}=\mathtt{C}\langle t_{0}^{\prime}\rangle
and
t_{1}=\mathtt{C}\langle t_{1}^{\prime}\rangle
,
where
t_{0}^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\pi}t_{1}^{\prime}
is a root step.
We reason by induction on
\mathtt{C}
.
We first consider the base cases where
\mathtt{C}=\Diamond
.

(1)

t_{0}^{\prime}=\lambda y.t[x\triangleleft u]\mapsto_{\pi}(\lambda y.t)[x\triangleleft u]=t_{1}^{\prime}
,
where
y\notin\operatorname{fv}(u)
.
There are two possible typing derivations.

(a)

The typing derivation is of the form

\Phi=\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};y:\mathcal{N};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\tau\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}{\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u};y:\mathcal{N}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u]:\tau}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\vdash\lambda y.t[x\triangleleft u]:\mathcal{N}\to\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({abs})}}\quad\textsc{Right=({cut})}

We construct the following derivation.

\Psi=\frac{\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};y:\mathcal{N};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\tau}{\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\lambda y.t:\mathcal{N}\to\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({abs})}\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\vdash(\lambda y.t)[x\triangleleft u]:\mathcal{N}\to\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}

Moreover,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)&=&\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)+(1,m,0)\\
&=&\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+(1,m,0)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(\lambda y.t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)\\
&=&\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right).\end{array}

(b)

The typing derivation is of the form

\Phi=\frac{}{\vdash\lambda y.t[x\triangleleft u]:\mathtt{a}}\quad\textsc{right=({ans}),vcenter}

We construct the following derivation that has the same measure.

\Psi=\frac{\frac{}{\vdash\lambda y.t:\mathtt{a}}\quad\textsc{right=({ans})}\frac{}{\vdash u:[\,]}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\vdash(\lambda y.t)[x\triangleleft u]:\mathtt{a}}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}

(2)

t_{0}^{\prime}=t[x\triangleleft u]s\mapsto_{\pi}(ts)[x\triangleleft u]=t_{1}^{\prime}
,
where
x\notin\operatorname{fv}(s)
.
The typing derivation is of the form

\Phi=\frac{\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u]:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}\Phi_{s}\rhd\Delta_{s}\vdash s:\mathcal{N}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u]s:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({app}),vcenter}

We construct the following derivation.

\Psi=\frac{\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma\Phi_{s}\rhd\Delta_{s}\vdash s:\mathcal{N}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{s};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash ts:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({app})}\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}}{(ts)[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma}}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}

Moreover, since
\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)=\operatorname{lv}_{x}(ts)
,

\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s},m\right)+(1,m,0)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(ts)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right).

(3)

t_{0}^{\prime}=ts[x\triangleleft u]\mapsto_{\pi}(ts)[x\triangleleft u]=t_{1}^{\prime}
,
where
x\notin\operatorname{fv}(t)
.
Let

\Phi_{s[x\triangleleft u]}=\frac{\left(\frac{\Phi^{i}_{s}\rhd\Delta_{s}^{i};x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash s:\rho_{i}\frac{\left(\Phi_{u}^{i,j}\rhd\Delta_{u}^{i,j}\vdash u:\delta_{j}\right)_{{j\in J_{i}}}}{\Delta_{u}^{i}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}_{i}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Delta_{u}^{i}\uplus\Delta_{s}^{i}\vdash s[x\triangleleft u]:\rho_{i}}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash s[x\triangleleft u]:\mathcal{N}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many}),vcenter}

The typing derivation
\Phi
is of the form

\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma\Phi_{s[x\triangleleft u]}\rhd\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash s[x\triangleleft u]:\mathcal{N}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash ts[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({app}),vcenter}

where
\mathcal{M}_{i}=[\delta_{j}]_{{j\in J_{i}}}
,

\mathcal{N}=[\rho_{i}]_{{i\in I}}
,

\Delta_{u}^{i}=\uplus_{{j\in J_{i}}}\Delta_{u}^{i,j}
,

\Delta_{u}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{u}^{i}
,
and
\Delta_{s}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{s}^{i}
.

Now, let

\Phi_{s}=\frac{\frac{\left(\Phi_{s}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{s}^{i};x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash s:\rho_{i}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{s};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash s:\mathcal{N}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{s};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash ts:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({app}),vcenter}\Phi_{u}=\frac{\left(\Phi_{u}^{i,j}\rhd\Delta_{u}^{i,j}\vdash u:\delta_{j}\right)_{{j\in J_{i}},{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many}),vcenter}

We construct the following derivation
\Psi
.

\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma\Phi_{s}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{s};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash ts:\sigma\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta_{u}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash(ts)[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}

where
\mathcal{M}=\sqcup_{{i\in I}}\mathcal{M}_{i}
, so that
\mathcal{M}=[\delta_{j}]_{{j\in J_{i}},{i\in I}}
.
Moreover, because
\operatorname{lv}_{x}(s)=\operatorname{lv}_{x}(ts)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+(1,m,0)

\displaystyle+\sum_{{i\in I}}\left(\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s}^{i},m\right)+\sum_{{j\in J_{i}}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u}^{i,j},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(s)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)

(4)

t_{0}^{\prime}=t[x\triangleleft u[y\triangleleft s]]\mapsto_{\pi}t[x\triangleleft u][y\triangleleft s]=t_{1}^{\prime}
,
where
y\notin\operatorname{fv}(t)
.
Let

\Phi_{u[y\triangleleft s]}=\frac{\left(\frac{\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{u}^{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\vdash u:\rho_{i}\frac{\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j}\rhd\Delta_{s}^{i,j}\vdash s:\delta_{j}\right)_{{j\in J_{i}}}}{\Delta_{s}^{i}\vdash s:\mathcal{N}_{i}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Delta_{u}^{i}\uplus\Delta_{s}^{i}\vdash u[y\triangleleft s]:\rho_{i}}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash u[y\triangleleft s]:\mathcal{M}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many}),vcenter}

The typing derivation
\Phi
is of the form

\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\sigma\Phi_{u[y\triangleleft s]}\rhd\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash u[y\triangleleft s]:\mathcal{M}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u[y\triangleleft s]]:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}

where
\mathcal{M}=[\rho_{i}]_{{i\in I}}
,
\mathcal{N}_{i}=[\delta_{j}]_{{j\in J_{i}}}
,

\Delta_{u}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{u}^{i}
,
\Delta_{s}^{i}=\uplus_{{j\in J_{i}}}\Delta_{s}^{i,j}
, and

\Delta_{s}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{s}^{i}
.

Now, let

\Phi_{t[x\triangleleft u]}=\frac{\Phi_{t}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{u}^{i};y:\mathcal{N}_{i}\vdash u:\rho_{i}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{u};y:\mathcal{N}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u};y:\mathcal{N}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({cut}),vcenter}

We then construct the following derivation
\Psi
.

\frac{\Phi_{t[x\triangleleft u]}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u};y:\mathcal{N}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j}\rhd\Delta_{s}^{i,j}\vdash s:\delta_{j}\right)_{{j\in J_{i}},{i\in I}}}{\Delta_{s}\vdash s:\mathcal{N}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta_{u}\uplus\Delta_{s}\vdash t[x\triangleleft u][y\triangleleft s]:\sigma}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}

where
\mathcal{N}=\sqcup_{{i\in I}}\mathcal{N}_{i}
, so that

\mathcal{N}=[\delta_{j}]_{{j\in J_{i}},{i\in I}}
. Moreover, because
y\notin\operatorname{fv}(t)
, we have that

\operatorname{lv}_{y}(t[x\triangleleft u])=\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{lv}_{y}(u)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])
if
y\in\operatorname{fv}(u)
, and

\operatorname{lv}_{y}(t[x\triangleleft u])=0
otherwise.
Now, we show that
\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])+\operatorname{lv}_{y}(u)+\operatorname{ES}([y\triangleleft s])\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j},m+\operatorname{lv}_{y}(t[x\triangleleft u])+\operatorname{ES}([y\triangleleft s])\right)
.
If
y\in\operatorname{fv}(u)
, this is immediate.
Otherwise, by the Relevance Lemma
23
we
have
J_{i}=[\,]
for any
i
thus
s
is not typed,
so that both measures are equal to (0,0,0).
Then,

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+\sum_{{i\in I}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u}^{i},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{{i\in I}}\sum_{{j\in J_{i}}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])+\operatorname{lv}_{y}(u)+\operatorname{ES}([y\triangleleft s])\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{t},m\right)+\sum_{{i\in I}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u}^{i},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{{i\in I}}\sum_{{j\in J_{i}}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{s}^{i,j},m+\operatorname{lv}_{y}(t[x\triangleleft u])+\operatorname{ES}([y\triangleleft s])\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)

Now, we analyse all the
inductive cases:

(1)

If
\mathtt{C}=\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, then
we have
\sigma=\mathcal{M}\to\tau
and
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma;x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o\rangle:\tau
.
By the i.h. there is
\Psi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma;x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle:\tau

and therefore
\Psi\rhd\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle:\tau
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi^{\prime},m\right)+(1,m,0)=_{\textit{i.h.}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi^{\prime},m\right)+(1,m,0)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)
.

(2)

If
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}u
, then
we have
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o\rangle:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma

and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{N}
.
By the i.h. there is
\Psi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle:\mathcal{N}\to\sigma
,
so
\Psi\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle u:\sigma
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi^{\prime},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m\right)+(1,m,0)=_{\textit{i.h.}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi^{\prime},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m\right)+(1,m,0)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)
.

(3)

If
\mathtt{C}=u\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
, the case is similar.

(4)

If
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[x\triangleleft u]
, then
we have
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o\rangle:\sigma

and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
.
By the i.h. there is

\Psi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle:\sigma
,
so
\Psi\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle[x\triangleleft u]:\sigma
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi^{\prime},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)=_{\textit{i.h.}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi^{\prime},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(t)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)
.

(5)

If
\mathtt{C}=u[x\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
, then
we have
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta;x:\mathcal{M}\vdash u:\sigma

and
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o\rangle:\mathcal{M}
.
By the i.h. there is

\Psi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle:\mathcal{M}
,
so
\Psi\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta\vdash u[x\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle o^{\prime}\rangle]:\sigma
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi,m\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi^{\prime},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(u)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)=_{\textit{i.h.}}\mathsf{M}\left(\Phi_{u},m\right)+\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi^{\prime},m+\operatorname{lv}_{x}(u)+\operatorname{ES}([x\triangleleft u])\right)=\mathsf{M}\left(\Psi,m\right)
.

\ndlrnormtyp

*

Proof A.11
.

First, we show that if
t
is an answer
\mathtt{L}\langle\lambda x.p\rangle
, we can type it with type
\mathtt{a}
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
.
We reason by induction on
\mathtt{L}
. If
\mathtt{L}=\Diamond
, this is immediate.
Otherwise, using the induction hypothesis, we build:

\frac{\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{L}\langle\lambda x.p\rangle:\mathtt{a}\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{L}\langle\lambda x.p\rangle[y\triangleleft u]:\mathtt{a}}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}

The statement is then trivial since
\Gamma=\emptyset
.
For neutral terms, we use induction on
\overline{\texttt{Ne}}
with a stronger hypothesis:
there exists a derivation for any given type
\tau
.

•

t=x
. We can build
\Phi\rhd x:[\tau]\vdash x:\tau
. Note that
x\in\operatorname{ndv}(t)
.

•

t=t^{\prime}u
, where
t^{\prime}\in\overline{\texttt{Ne}}
.
By the i.h. there is a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:[\,]\to\tau
verifying the statement.
We then build:

\frac{\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}:[\,]\to\tau\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}u:\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({app})}

The statement holds by the i.h. because
\operatorname{ndv}(t)=\operatorname{ndv}(t^{\prime})
.

•

t=t^{\prime}[x\triangleleft u]
, where
t^{\prime}\in\overline{\texttt{Ne}}
.
By the i.h. there is a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{t^{\prime}}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau

verifying the statement.
Let
\Gamma_{t^{\prime}}=\Gamma^{\prime};x:[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}
.
There are two cases.

–

If
x\notin\operatorname{ndv}(t^{\prime})
, then by the i.h.
I=[\,]
.
We can then build the following derivation.

\frac{\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau:\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t^{\prime}[x\triangleleft u]:\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}

The property holds for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}
because
\operatorname{ndv}(t)=\operatorname{ndv}(t^{\prime})
.

–

Otherwise,
t=t^{\prime}[x/u]
, and
u\in\overline{\texttt{Ne}}
. We apply
the i.h. on u.
There are derivations
\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\sigma_{i}
.
We take
\Gamma=\Gamma_{t^{\prime}}\uplus_{i\in I}\Delta_{i}
and we build:

\frac{\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{t^{\prime}}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau\frac{(\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\sigma_{i})}{\uplus_{i\in I}\Delta_{i}\vdash u:[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}}\quad\textsc{Right=({many})}}{\Gamma\vdash t^{\prime}[x/u]:\tau}\quad\textsc{right=({cut})}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus_{i\in I}\Delta_{i}
.
Moreover,
\operatorname{ndv}(t)=(\operatorname{ndv}(t^{\prime})\setminus x)\cup\operatorname{ndv}(u)
so the second property holds on
\Gamma
by the two induction hypothesis.

\partialasub

Proof A.12
.

By induction on the structure of
\mathtt{C}
.

•

If
\mathtt{C}=\Diamond
then the property trivially holds taking

\Gamma^{\prime}=\emptyset
,
\Delta=\Gamma
,
\mathcal{M}=[\sigma]
,

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd x:[\sigma]\vdash x:\sigma
and
\Phi_{u}=\Phi
.

•

If
\mathtt{C}=\lambda y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
then
y\notin\operatorname{fv}(u)
and by
\alpha
-conversion we can assume that
x\neq y
. There are two cases:

(1)

If
\Phi=\frac{\Phi_{0}\rhd\Gamma;y:\mathcal{M}_{y}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\tau}{\Gamma\vdash\lambda y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\mathcal{M}_{y}\to\tau}
then by
i.h. there are

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta,\mathcal{M},\Phi^{\prime}_{0}
and
\Phi_{u}
such that

\Gamma;y:\mathcal{M}_{y}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\uplus\Delta
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau
and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
.
By the Relevance
23

y\notin\operatorname{dom}(\Delta)
thus
\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}=\Gamma^{\prime};y:\mathcal{M}_{y}
. Therefore,

\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}=(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M});y:\mathcal{M}_{y}
and

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\rhd(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M});y:\mathcal{M}_{y}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau}{\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\lambda y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\mathcal{M}_{y}\to\tau}

(2)

If
\Phi=\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash\lambda y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\mathtt{a}}
then
taking
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{M}=[\,]
and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]
we have

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash\lambda y.\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\mathtt{a}}

•

If
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}t
then
\Phi=\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\sigma\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash t:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}{\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle t:\sigma}
, where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
. By i.h. there are

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},\Delta,\mathcal{M},\Phi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\Phi_{u}
such that

\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus\Delta
,

\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\sigma
and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
.
Therefore, taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
we have

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}+x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\sigma\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash t:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}{(\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus x:\mathcal{M})\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle t:\sigma}

where
(\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus x:\mathcal{M})\uplus\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}
.

•

If
\mathtt{C}=t\mathtt{C}^{\prime}
then
\Phi
is of the form

\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash t:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}\to\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}\right)_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}:}}{\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash t\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma}

where
\Gamma_{2}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Gamma_{i}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
. There are two cases:

(1)

If
I\neq\emptyset
then by i.h.
\exists\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
,

\exists\Delta_{i}
,
\exists\mathcal{M}_{i}
,
\exists\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
,
\exists\Phi_{u}^{i}

s.t.
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus\Delta_{i}
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}
and
\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}_{i}
,
for all
{i\in I}
. Let
\Delta=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{i}
and
\mathcal{M}=\sqcup_{{i\in I}}\mathcal{M}_{i}

then from Split
24
we have
\Phi_{u}=\frac{\left(\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}_{i}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}
. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
then
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\uplus\Delta=\Gamma_{2}
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is defined by

\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash t:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}\to\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}\right)_{i\in I}}{\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}}}{(\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime}_{2})\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}
.

(2)

If
I=\emptyset
, then
[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}=[\,]
,
\Gamma_{2}=\emptyset
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
. Therefore, taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}
,

\Delta=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{M}=[\,]
,
\Phi_{u}=\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]}
,
we have
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}\uplus x:[\,]=\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:[\,]
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\emptyset=\Gamma
.
We take

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash t:[\,]\to\sigma\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:[\,]}{\Gamma_{1}\vdash t\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma}.

•

If
\mathtt{C}=\mathtt{C}^{\prime}[y\triangleleft t]
then
\Phi=\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1};y:\mathcal{M}_{y}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash t:\mathcal{M}_{y}}{\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle[y\triangleleft t]:\sigma}
where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
. Moreover,
y\notin\operatorname{fv}(u)
and by
\alpha
-conversion we
can assume that
x\neq y
.
By i.h. there are
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},\Delta,\mathcal{M},\Phi^{\prime}_{1}
and
\Phi_{u}
such that
\Gamma_{1};y:\mathcal{M}_{y}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus\Delta
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma
and
\Phi_{u}\rhd\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
.
By the Relevance
23

y\notin\operatorname{dom}(\Delta)
thus
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime};y:\mathcal{M}_{y}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M});y:\mathcal{M}_{y}
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Delta=\Gamma_{1}
. Therefore, taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
we have

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{\Phi^{\prime}_{1}\rhd(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M});y:\mathcal{M}_{y}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\sigma\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash t:\mathcal{M}_{y}}{(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M})\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle[y\triangleleft t]:\sigma}

where
(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M})\uplus\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}
.

•

If
\mathtt{C}=t[y\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}]
then
\Phi
is of the form

\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1};y:[\tau_{i}]_{i\in I}\vdash t:\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}\right)_{i\in I}}{\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle:[\tau_{i}]_{i\in I}}}{\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}\vdash t[y\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle u\rangle\!\rangle]:\sigma}

where
\Gamma_{2}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Gamma_{i}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma_{2}
. There are two cases:

(1)

If
I\neq\emptyset
then by i.h. there are

\Gamma^{\prime}_{i},\Delta_{i},\mathcal{M}_{i},\Phi^{\prime}_{i}
and
\Phi_{u}^{i}

such that
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus\Delta_{i}
,
\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}
and
\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}_{i}
,
for all
{i\in I}
. Let
\Delta=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Delta_{i}
and
\mathcal{M}=\sqcup_{{i\in I}}\mathcal{M}_{i}

then from Split
24
we have
\Phi_{u}=\frac{\left(\Phi_{u}^{i}\rhd\Delta_{i}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}_{i}\right)_{{i\in I}}}{\Delta\vdash u:\mathcal{M}}
. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}=\uplus_{{i\in I}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
then
\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\uplus\Delta=\Gamma_{2}
and
\Phi^{\prime}
is defined by

\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1};y:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}\vdash t:\sigma\frac{\left(\Phi^{\prime}_{i}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}_{i}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:\tau_{i}\right)_{i\in I}}{\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}}}{(\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime}_{2})\uplus x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t[y\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle]:\sigma}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}
.

(2)

If
I=\emptyset
then
[\tau_{i}]_{{i\in I}}=[\,]
,
\Gamma_{2}=\emptyset
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
. Moreover,
y\notin\operatorname{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
.
Therefore, taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}
,
\Delta=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{M}=[\,]
,
\Phi_{u}=\frac{}{\emptyset\vdash u:[\,]}
,
we have
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}\uplus x:[\,]=\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus x:[\,]
and

\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Delta=\Gamma_{1}\uplus\emptyset=\Gamma
.
We take

\Phi^{\prime}=\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash t:\sigma\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle:[\,]}{\Gamma_{1}\vdash t[y\triangleleft\mathtt{C}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle x\rangle\!\rangle]:\sigma}
